It collapsed because of the fear that if hon members sitting here had had their say, they would have been supportive of the motion which was introduced by this side of the House.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! I think that perhaps the hon member is casting an unfair reflection on my ruling of yesterday.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#mopp">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P A S MOPP</person>:</from>

<p>Sir, I am not attacking that ruling; it was within the rules and absolutely correct.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Thank you, I appreciate that. I only acted in terms of the rules according to which I have to work.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#mopp">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P A S MOPP</person>:</from>

<p>Sir, the ruling was absolutely correct according to the rules, but accusations, which should have been refuted but were not, were levelled from this side of the House. The people at large who read Hansard accept that those accusations are the truth. There was a golden opportunity to continue the debate until tomorrow.</p>

<p>The prevailing fear was that hon members would get up and wail and moan about the lack of things in their constituencies, and in this way would in fact support our motion. That this is why this debate was cut short in this manner by hon members on that side of the House.</p>

<p>The time will come, however, when we will afford every hon member on that side of the House the time and opportunity to unburden himself about the shortcomings in his constituency. We shall find that there are many shortcomings. We do not agree, therefore, with the reasons advanced here by the hon the Leader of the House. The poor hon Leader of the House must have sucked them out of his thumb.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_house">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>I am not poor; I am never poor!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#mopp">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P A S MOPP</person>:</from>

<p>I thank the hon the Leader of the House for saying that he is not poor. He is rich, because he is getting the unpaid holidays he <span class="col_561-562" refersTo="page_0303"/>proposed. [Interjections.] I support the amendment proposed by the UDP, since the interests of the people at large should receive precedence in this House. We were sent to this House in the interests of the people in the first place.</p>

<p>A coup d&#x2019;&#x00E9;tat was executed in Bophuthatswana today. The coup in the Transkei was condoned. Is the SA Defence Force not behind all these coups? A coup has been executed in Bophuthatswana now&#x2014;to cover up the coup which was executed in the Transkei. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>Mr Chairman, I am going to ask your ruling on what hon members are saying &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! I permitted the hon member for Border to refer to the coup, because it was mentioned in the statement. The hon member was justified in referring to it.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#mopp">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P A S MOPP</person>:</from>

<p>We want to know why the coup in the Transkei was supported by this Government.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! No, I permitted the hon member to refer to the coup because it was mentioned in the statement and because he said it was one of the reasons why they had wanted the House to sit, but we are not discussing the coup as such at the moment.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#mopp">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P A S MOPP</person>:</from>

<p>Sir, we could have had a debate on this coup today. Why was this statement made? Why must we simply swallow everything we are told? Why can we not debate that statement in this House today?</p>

<p>Another thing that is of concern here and in the Cape community in particular, is the murder of our children. We read in the newspapers that eight children have now been murdered in this region. When one switches on the television, and sees that a White person has been murdered, it seems almost as if the entire Police Force, the Defence Force and the Navy have been called in to investigate the case. Why can they not also bring the murderer of our children on the Cape Flats to book? This is the kind of thing we want to know.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>*An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>Talk about the coup, man.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#mopp">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P A S MOPP</person>:</from>

<p>Sir, you are the kindest person I know; you are a real gentleman. I shall rather elaborate on these matters later when we discuss the Budget Vote. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>Why can a better effort not be made to arrest that murderer? We as members of this House demand that the police do everything in their power to arrest that murderer before he murders another one of our children. This is the kind of thing affecting our community that we should voice here. That is why I am opposing the motion before the House.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#lockey">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">D LOCKEY</person>:</from>

<p>If you want to have &#x2026; [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon member for Border may continue.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#mopp">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P A S MOPP</person>:</from>

<p>I just want to warn the hon member, Sir, that if he wants to leave today, he only has to fool around with me; we shall force him to leave again.</p>

<p>Sir, a slaughter of human beings is taking place. I am referring to the Pietermaritzburg area. Hundreds of people have been murdered already. Some of them have been burnt. These are the things we have to discuss in this House. Children of the police are being shot and killed.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Yes, but the hon member will have an opportunity to discuss this; he will not be forbidden to do so.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#mopp">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P A S MOPP</person>:</from>

<p>Sir, we could still have discussed all these things this week. These are important matters. Yesterday half a page of <i>The Argus</i> was devoted to the names of students and teachers who are in detention at the moment.</p>

<p>&#x2020;We want to know whether the hon the Minister of Education and Culture in this House is responsible for those detentions. Those are things that we wanted to discuss, but now everything has been cut short so that those hon members can have a glorified holiday&#x2014;and an unpaid one at that!</p>

<p>I support the amendment.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, you know I appreciate every word that has been said by hon members of this House who have participated in this debate. That, unfortunately, is the price one has to pay for democracy. I certainly appreciate the concern of hon members <span class="col_563-564" refersTo="page_0304"/>in the opposition for the state of this country. However, Sir, these hon members had a golden opportunity yesterday to place all these things on record, to spell this out quite clearly to those on the inside, and particularly to those people on the outside. However, what did they do to their hon leader? They dropped him! If that were ever to happen to me, I would know what to do with them!</p>

<p>Mr Chairman, a leader is as strong as those people around him. I pity the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition with all his strength and the weakness of those around him. [Interjections.] Those hon members had an opportunity yesterday after the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition had stated his case&#x2014;which he took more than two hours to do! Yet, when I replied&#x2014; which I did&#x2014;in 25 minutes, not a single hon member of his party was prepared to endorse a single word he had said. Now they say we want to go on holiday.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#mopp">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P A S MOPP</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, surely the hon the Leader of the House is misleading us, because the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition had put the question and it was accepted by the other side.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Sir, it does appear as though some of us are slower in learning than others. I am not responsible for that, however.</p>

<p>Mr Chairman, allow me to start off with the hon member for Durban Suburbs. He says&#x2014;and thank goodness he says this&#x2014;that he does not understand my politics. That is why we cannot walk this road together, because he will never be able to understand the road of democracy. The very same hon member for Durban Suburbs will phone me up in the middle of the night and say: &#x201C;Please, Miley, I made a mistake today. Two Bills were passed when you were not there, and I had not even realised that they had put the question. Will you kindly stop them?&#x201D; This is why I say the hon member must please go his own way. I do not need him here.</p>

<p>Sir, this is a House of Parliament. Here one&#x2019;s word is one&#x2019;s honour. People have missed the presidency, the premiership of this country because they told lies. I would like to remind hon members that in this Chamber one&#x2019;s word should be one&#x2019;s honour.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#redcliffe">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">C R REDCLIFFE</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I rise on a point of order: Is the hon the Leader of the House implying that an hon member of the opposition told a lie?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I shall answer that question: If the cap fits, wear it!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! No, I do not think that the hon the Leader of the House is implying that.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I am merely making a point. The Whips, as well as the Leaders of the House come together and organise a week&#x2019;s work. The Whip of the Official Opposition, the hon member for Durban Suburbs, was there at the Whips&#x2019; meeting together with the hon the Chief Whip of Parliament when we organised the work for this week, and agreed.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#kippen">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">C J KIPPEN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I rise on a point of order.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_house">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>I have not even mentioned what he did!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Could the hon member for Durban Suburbs just wait until the hon the Leader of the House has completed his statement. After that I will give the hon member an opportunity to ask a question.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_house">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Sir, I do not understand what is going on here.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! No, start again.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Sir, he was there when we agreed that the matters on the Order Paper would be debated next week. He, and all hon members know what kind of preparation it takes to place an item on the agenda and to prepare for it to be debated.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#kippen">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">C J KIPPEN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, on a point of order: The hon the Leader of the House is misleading this House in that although next week&#x2019;s work was discussed, no mention was made of the fact that the no-confidence debate would be as short as it was. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! That is not a point of order. The hon the Leader of the House may proceed.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Let me now thank the next member who took part in the debate, the hon member for Schauderville. He comes to this Chamber when he is in Cape Town, and I thank him for coming this time.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#redcliffe">

<from><span class="col_565-566" refersTo="page_0305"/>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">C R REDCLIFFE</person>:</from>

<p>I do not need your patronising.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>At regular intervals those benches are totally empty, but it is their right to stay away. He warns me, as the Leader of the House&#x2014;it is his democratic right to warn me&#x2014;that he is going to object to motions which I introduce here. However, I will enjoy this, because&#x2014;as hon members will notice&#x2014;I enjoy talking. So, he will be giving me an opportunity to introduce these motions continuously and to make the points that have to be made. He must please carry on with this trend; we will enjoy this.</p>

<p>However, if the hon member gives me his word&#x2014; he will remember that he gave me his word last year that he would oppose those two Bills &#x2026; [Interjections.] He was sitting on that bench when he gave me his word, and that was what his word was worth. The moment the issue was raised here, his word was not worth the dust on my shoes. [Interjections.] We take a man&#x2019;s word for what it is worth. So, if he gives me his word that he warns me, I accept that as well.</p>

<p>*Mr Chairman, hon members referred to the houses that are being built for the Ministers&#x2014; they raised the matter, not I; I have seen them also building houses there for the Ministers&#x2014;and they objected to people occupying those houses, but I have never heard them complaining about the fact that the hon the Minister of Foreign Affairs and other hon Ministers occupy houses provided by the State.</p>

<p>&#x2020;What is more, all of them have an opportunity to demonstrate their objection to this. All they have to do is to move out of housing provided by the State. They do not have to live in Laboria Park. If they do that, the people outside will see how honest they are in their approach.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#mckenzie">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P C MCKENZIE</person>:</from>

<p>Accept the challenge! [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>If hon members will allow me &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon the Leader of the House is replying to the debate. I called the hon member for Schauderville to order when he said that the Ministers were building houses for themselves, because the Ministers do not build the houses themselves. The hon the Leader of the House may proceed.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_house">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Sir, I accept your ruling, but people are saying that houses are being built for the Ministers and the hon member feels that the Ministers should not be entitled to live in those houses. In that case, surely, no Ministers should occupy houses provided by the State. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>&#x2020;However, as I have said, they have this opportunity of demonstrating their sincerity. [Interjections.] Over the years this party has demonstrated its sincerity. When those houses were built in Belhar, we did not move into those houses, because we associated ourselves with the people. [Interjections.] I want to challenge the hon member for Schauderville, as he is so concerned about the homeless, to move out of his house in Laboria Park; but he must first settle the account, and then we can get people who are homeless to move into his house.</p>

<p>The hon member for Macassar is one gentleman in this Chamber whom I admired. I regarded him as a person whom it was worth listening to. Not only did he have the gift of the gab, but his use of words was excellent. However, as one listened to him this afternoon, one realized that the charisma of the man had disappeared, because he was putting a case which was so unconvincing.</p>

<p>I want to tell the hon member for Border that I expected more of him. I know that hon member well. He is a person who grasps an opportunity and that opportunity was right there yesterday. I put it to him that the reason he did not enter the debate yesterday, was that he was ashamed of what his leader had said.</p>

<p>Question put,</p>

<p>Upon which the House divided.</p>

<p>As fewer than fifteen members (viz T R George, C B Herandien, F G Herwels, C J Kippen, P A S Mopp, P J Muller, J A Rabie and C R Redcliffe) appeared on one side,</p>

<p>Question declared affirmed and amendment dropped.</p>

</speech>

</debateSection>

<debateSection name="#adjournment_of_house">

<heading>ADJOURNMENT OF HOUSE</heading>

<summary>(Motion)</summary>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_house">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I move:</p>

<block name="quote">That the House do now adjourn.</block>

<p>Agreed to.</p>

</speech>

<adjournment>

<p>The House adjourned at <recordedTime time="1988-02-10T16:47:00">16h47</recordedTime> until after the disposal of the business of the Joint Sitting on Monday.</p>

</adjournment>

</debateSection>

</debateSection>

<debateSection name="#proceedings_of_the_house_of_delegates">

<heading><span class="col_567-568" refersTo="page_0306"/><i>PROCEEDINGS OF THE HOUSE OF DELEGATES</i></heading>

<prayers>

<narrative>Prayers&#x2014;<recordedTime time="1988-02-10T15:30:00">15h30.</recordedTime></narrative>

</prayers>

<debateSection name="#tabling_of_bill">

<heading>TABLING OF BILL</heading>

<p>The ACTING SPEAKER laid upon the Table:</p>

<block name="quote"><i>Part Appropriation Bill</i> [B 36&#x2014;88 (GA)].</block>

</debateSection>

<debateSection name="#report_of_standing_committee">

<heading>REPORT OF STANDING COMMITTEE</heading>

<p>The ACTING SPEAKER laid upon the Table the First Report of the Standing Committee on Provincial Affairs: Transvaal, dated 10 February 1988, as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">The Standing Committee on Provincial Affairs: Transvaal, having considered a draft proclamation seeking to amend the Municipal Elections Ordinance, 1970 (Ordinance 16 of 1970), referred to it on 29 January 1988 in terms of Rule 22A, begs to report that it has approved the proclamation.</block>

</debateSection>

<debateSection name="#new_member">

<heading>NEW MEMBER</heading>

<p>The CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE announced that the casual vacancy in the seat of a member elected in terms of section 43 (1) (c) of the Republic of South Africa Constitution Act, 1983, had been filled with effect from 9 February 1988 by the election of Mr A S Razak.</p>

</debateSection>

<debateSection name="#affirmation">

<heading>AFFIRMATION</heading>

<p>Mr A S Razak, introduced by Mr B Dookie and Mr Y Moolla, made and subscribed the affirmation and took his seat.</p>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, on behalf of this side of the House I want to extend to the hon Mr Razak our congratulations for being elected as an indirectly elected member of this House in a fair and democratic ballot. Of course, I just joked that one could have extended the courtesy for one member of this side of the House also to have directed him into the House. Nevertheless, we have had quite a long association with the hon Mr Razak and once again I want to offer him my congratulations and good wishes. On behalf of the members of the Ministers&#x2019; Council I extend to him all the co-operation we can possibly give. Iam sure that he will be able to make a contribution to enhance the image of this House.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_members">

<from><person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBERS</person>:</from>

<p>Hear, hear!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I appreciate the steps that the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council took to introduce and welcome the new hon member. I hope the spirit in which he did this will prevail throughout our deliberations in this House.</p>

<p>On behalf of my colleagues and myself and all those people who supported our nominee and voted for him, I should like to thank you all very sincerely and I wish the hon member Mr Razak well.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, on behalf of the PRP I should like to associate myself with the congratulatory sentiments expressed. I agree with the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition that the election of the hon member Mr Razak yesterday serves as another example of how, occasionally, hon members on both sides of the House, in a co-operative spirit, can go for quality. In this hon member we have an example of very good quality and I feel sure that the hon member Mr Razak will endeavour to uphold that sentiment which you, Mr Chairman, expressed in the prayer this afternoon, namely to maintain the trust that people have placed in members of Parliament. To maintain that trust, of course, he will regard with contempt any carrots that may be offered to him. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! On behalf of all hon members of the House of Delegates I wish to welcome the hon member for the Eastern Transvaal. It is my prayer that God will give him strength, wisdom, knowledge, patience and understanding as a member of Parliament.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, the hon member is a nominated member. He lives in the Eastern Transvaal.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! He is an indirectly elected member. Thank you for the correction.</p>

</speech>

</debateSection>

<debateSection name="#calling_of_joint_sitting">

<heading>CALLING OF JOINT SITTING</heading>

<p>The CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE announced that the Acting Speaker had called a joint sitting of the three Houses of Parliament for Monday, 15 <span class="col_569-570" refersTo="page_0307"/>February, at 14h15 for the delivering of Second Reading speeches on certain Bills.</p>

</debateSection>

<debateSection name="#resumption_of_proceedings_on_bill">

<heading>RESUMPTION OF PROCEEDINGS ON BILL</heading>

<summary>(Motion)</summary>

<p>The LEADER OF THE HOUSE moved without notice: That in terms of Rule 40 the proceedings on the <i>Public Accountants&#x2019; and Auditors&#x2019; Amendment Bill</i> [B 123A and B&#x2014;87 (GA)] be resumed from the stage reached during the preceding session.</p>

<p>Agreed to.</p>

</debateSection>

<debateSection name="#recommittal_of_bill_to_standing_committee">

<heading>RECOMMITTAL OF BILL TO STANDING COMMITTEE</heading>

<summary>(Motion)</summary>

<p>The MINISTER OF EDUCATION AND CULTURE (for the Minister of Education and Development Aid) moved without notice: That the Order for the Second Reading of the <i>Borders of Particular States Extension Amendment Bill</i> [B 9A and B&#x2014;88 (GA)] be discharged and the subject of the Bill be recommitted to the Standing Committee on Foreign Affairs and Development Aid.</p>

<p>Agreed to.</p>

</debateSection>

<debateSection name="#withdrawal_of_bill">

<heading>WITHDRAWAL OF BILL</heading>

<summary>(Motion)</summary>

<p>The Minister of Education and Culture (for the Minister of National Health and Population Development) moved: That the <i>Health Amendment Bill</i> [B 88&#x2014;87 (GA)] be withdrawn.</p>

<p>Agreed to.</p>

</debateSection>

<debateSection name="#debate_on_motion_of_no-confidence_in_the_ministers_council">

<heading>DEBATE ON MOTION OF NO-CONFIDENCE IN THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</heading>

<summary>(Resumed)</summary>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! During the course of yesterday&#x2019;s debate the hon member for Reservoir Hills raised a point of order regarding certain allegations that the hon member Mr Thaver had made in his speech. I then indicated that I would study the Hansard transcript and give a ruling today. I have now had the opportunity of studying the unrevised transcript of the hon member Mr Thaver&#x2019;s speech and I am quite satisfied that the hon member Mr Thaver&#x2019;s words can be interpreted as an accusation that the hon member for Reservoir Hills was a party to the drawing up of false affidavits and I therefore have no choice but to kindly request the hon member to withdraw that statement.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#thaver">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M THAVER</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I accept your ruling and I withdraw it.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodley">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">K MOODLEY</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, in the few minutes I have left, I want to talk about the question of bribery and corruption which has been an issue since 1985. It did not start in 1985, however. It started long before that, before the House of Delegates even existed. It was in 1985, however, that it was brought to this House, and it has been debated ever since. I think it is important that accusations should be substantiated and dealt with properly and in their place.</p>

<p>People in positions of power must always remember that they are accountable to the people who put them in those positions. It is unlike a position of financial or any other strength. Power is different. It is like an electric stream. One cannot see it, but one can use it and abuse it. Power is a good servant, and it can be a very dangerous master. People who hold high positions of power must remember that from time to time many accusations will be made against them. It is not easy to hold positions of power in public life. I have held public positions, and I have been accused.</p>

<p>Let me give hon members my own example. When I held a position of power I was accused of various things. This went on for some time from the oppositions in my area. I requested the administrator to appoint a commission to investigate me. One cannot let people point fingers at one and still hold a position of power. If one knows one has nothing to hide, one just submits oneself for an investigation. That is what I did. Messrs Wilson, Cross and Hagarth sat as commissioners and conducted an investigation for two days. At the end they found nothing that could be pinned on me. They could not hang anything on me; in fact they complimented me for bringing the matter up and clearing my name. I am not saying that the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council is guilty of anything at all.</p>

<p>I will be standing on his side in fact to help him clear his name.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#thaver">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M THAVER</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, will the hon member take a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodley">

<from><span class="col_571-572" refersTo="page_0308"/>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">K MOODLEY</person>:</from>

<p>No, Mr Chairman, I have no time. That hon member should sit down and listen.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#thaver">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M THAVER</person>:</from>

<p>That is not the way for one hon member to address another!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodley">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">K MOODLEY</person>:</from>

<p>That hon member is supposed to be the hon the Deputy Chairman of Committees in this House but his behaviour leaves much to be desired.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#thaver">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M THAVER</person>:</from>

<p>He is talking nonsense!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, may I ask the hon member a question? Did he take the matter concerning the person who made allegations against him after the commission had cleared him any further?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodley">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">K MOODLEY</person>:</from>

<p>No, I did not take the matter any further because it is all recorded in Hansard. There is no point in taking it any further unless I want to sue them to make some money.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>No, the situation was different.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodley">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">K MOODLEY</person>:</from>

<p>We have certain examples. One of them was mine, the other was Fernando Marcos and the latest is Matanzima in the Transkei. There are times when people are guilty but there are other times when they are not. I believe that, until proved otherwise, the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council is not guilty. I will therefore ask him to submit himself to the investigation.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>I agree, but your situation was different.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodley">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">K MOODLEY</person>:</from>

<p>We will clear the whole matter for once and for all and we will be happy to do that. [Interjections.] I must say that the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council is spending a great deal of valuable time defending himself.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>No, I am attacking, not defending.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodley">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">K MOODLEY</person>:</from>

<p>Perhaps he is attacking, but he has stood accused for years. Why does he not clear his name so that we can all be happy?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>I want to clear myself, but not in the way that it is done here.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodley">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">K MOODLEY</person>:</from>

<p>The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council said in his speech the other day that the hon the State President will not just appoint a commission of inquiry. My understanding is that if this House has a majority vote asking the hon the State President to appoint a commission of inquiry, it will clear everybody here. It will clear the name of the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council as well as all of us. We can then start afresh and go ahead with the work that is the reason why we were put here. That is what we are supposed to do instead of fighting and arguing against each other as we have been doing for the past three years. It seems as though we are going to continue with this and that it is not going to stop.</p>

<p>I say that we should put the matter to a secret vote. [Interjections.] There are many persons on both sides of this House who are beginning to wonder why, if there is nothing to hide, we are not allowing a commission of inquiry. I agree that one cannot appoint a commission of inquiry for every little whisper that requests it but after these matters have been going on for so long it is high time that everything comes into the open. If there is nothing to hide, why is a commission of inquiry not requested? The guilty are afraid.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>The false accusers are afraid! [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodley">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">K MOODLEY</person>:</from>

<p>When there is a commission of inquiry they will be subpoenaed to give evidence, and their bluff will be called.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>It must happen outside, not under Parliamentary protection.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodley">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">K MOODLEY</person>:</from>

<p>We do not need protection. Call for a commission of inquiry. They will come and give evidence and the whole matter will be cleared.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>You are right when you say that they do not need protection&#x2014;I agree with you.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodley">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">K MOODLEY</person>:</from>

<p>They should not need protection. If one wants to accuse, one must be able to stand up and prove it. That is why we want a commission. If they do not prove it, they lose it. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from><span class="col_573-574" refersTo="page_0309"/>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, may I ask the hon member whether he will agree that not only one hon member in the House but a large number of hon members are consistently calling for the appointment of a judicial commission?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodley">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">K MOODLEY</person>:</from>

<p>I said earlier that for many years there has been a call for a commission of inquiry to clear the air. I did not only refer to one hon member. We have been going on for many years and I for one find it very embarrassing to sit here year in and year out listening to these matters.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, will the hon member agree that it is only I and no one else who since 1985 has been making this matter topical?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodley">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">K MOODLEY</person>:</from>

<p>I disagree. I disagree to the extent that accusations are made and then defences are put up. The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council is not the one who is raising it, and that is the fact of the matter. Things like the Blacklist, the education scandal and the land scandal come up often. He is not raising these matters, others are raising it.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>What about the Malgate scandal? Do you want me to blacken my face in Malgate? I shall not come to Malgate to blacken my face. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodley">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">K MOODLEY</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, one may ask what is different about Malgate, but when something goes wrong in a local authority &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>But you want me to hang for it.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodley">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">K MOODLEY</person>:</from>

<p>No, nobody is asking the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council to hang. He should not hang. It is his duty as Minister of Housing to do what he is supposed to do and he must never do anything that is illegal, because I shall be the first one to call upon him. Nobody must ask him to do anything illegal. I shall defend him to the hilt, because he must do the right thing. He has the power of the position and you must exercise it responsibly.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#dasoo">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">I C DASOO</person>:</from>

<p>[Inaudible.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodley">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">K MOODLEY</person>:</from>

<p>The hon member must go outside and get snuffed. He must take his snuffbox and go outside.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#dasoo">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">I C DASOO</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman is the hon member prepared to take a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodley">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">K MOODLEY</person>:</from>

<p>No, I have no time to take a question. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! May I ask hon members to stop interjecting and to give the hon member an opportunity to continue with his address. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, is the hon member prepared to take a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodley">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">K MOODLEY</person>:</from>

<p>No, Mr Chairman, my time is too limited.</p>

<p>I want to conclude my speech now. I must say that when people jump out of every corner, it must be hurting.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#lambat">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A E LAMBAT</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I have been listening very attentively to the debate on the motion of no-confidence in the Ministers&#x2019; Council. The major topic of the hon Leader of the Official Opposition and the hon member for Reservoir Hills has been inferences about individuals instead of the Ministers&#x2019; Council.</p>

<p>Yesterday while I was listening to the speech of the hon member for Reservoir Hills he asked who gave individuals the right to enter into agreement with two communities for land. It is all very well to speak with regard to matters concerning local authority programmes but we all know that we have to face the practicalities. As one of my colleagues has said, when he flies over Cape Town he sees the beautiful Table Mountain and wishes that it was in his hometown of Palm Ridge, but unfortunately it cannot be there because it is here. In the same way the Group Areas Act stands on the Statute Book and at present we have to do things in terms of this Act. We cannot get away from this. The reality of its existence is there.</p>

<p>With particular regard to the East Rand where the Indians and Coloureds have really been taken for a ride by the various municipalities, the arrangements were that Boksburg would house all the Coloureds of the East Rand and Benoni would house all the Indians of the East Rand. Had the Indian community not fought for this there would have been no other areas like Bakerton, Palm Ridge, Nigel and other areas, but because of the steadfastness of the Indian community this came about. I therefore see no wrong in two aggrieved communities coming to <span class="col_575-576" refersTo="page_0310"/>an agreement through the leaders of those two communities, for example their management committees and members of Parliament. When they come to an agreement there is nothing wrong with it.</p>

<p>We have a very abnormal situation in that the hon member for Reservoir Hills resigned from the President&#x2019;s Council because Blacks had not been taken into the legislative machinery. Blacks are still not part of the legislative machinery of the Government and the hon member for Reservoir Hills had to come back into Parliament. What is more, he had to hijack a party which I and the hon member Mr Abram and our associates formed, called Solidarity. He hijacked that party and pushed us out of it. He became its leader and in the end he had to leave it to form another party. That is the abnormal situation we have.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#lambat">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A E LAMBAT</person>:</from>

<p>I do not have time for questions because I have been given only 12 minutes to speak. If I am given more time to speak, I am prepared to answer questions.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, will the hon member for Actonville take a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon member is not prepared to take any questions.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#lambat">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A E LAMBAT</person>:</from>

<p>I have only 12 minutes and I do not have time to say what I want to say.</p>

<p>A great deal was said yesterday about why all other areas are being stifled and stopped. That is not the case. What is happening here is that one area has to be fully investigated and finalised before other areas can be dealt with because every time a mushroom area is investigated it prolongs the proclamation of the one area that is coming up. That sets the whole machinery back.</p>

<p>It may be that a monopoly is what Solidarity wants. [Interjections.] It may be a funny world that we live in or it may be a queer situation in that the more people talk about the bribery and corruption of the NPP, the more the public wants to vote for them. I do not know why.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>Does he wear Barclay shoes?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#lambat">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A E LAMBAT</person>:</from>

<p>Perhaps.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#bandulalla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M BANDULALLA</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I speak in peace. I do not want to become involved in a confrontation but would the hon member take a very simple question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon member is not prepared to take any questions.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#bandulalla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M BANDULALLA</person>:</from>

<p>From me? [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#lambat">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A E LAMBAT</person>:</from>

<p>I would not mind taking questions from anyone but I do not have the time. That is the only problem. Otherwise I would not mind answering hundreds of questions.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>Ask for the privilege of the half hour.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#lambat">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A E LAMBAT</person>:</from>

<p>The situation is that in the Transvaal there was only one elected Solidarity member, and in the last election he &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_members">

<from><person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBERS</person>:</from>

<p>There were two!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#lambat">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A E LAMBAT</person>:</from>

<p>There was only one. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>When we adjourned here last year there was only one elected member of Parliament for Solidarity.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>There were two!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#lambat">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A E LAMBAT</person>:</from>

<p>He received just over 1 300 votes in the 1984 election. What happened then was that with all the linking of bribery and corruption and the NPP, in this election those votes were reduced by half, to a mere 722, and the votes for the NPP increased to a majority of about 2 000 votes. This goes to show what the people are thinking about.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>How many dead people voted?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#lambat">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A E LAMBAT</person>:</from>

<p>I was most surprised that the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition had to make a personal appearance at a representation on behalf of the people of my constituency at the hearing of the Group Areas Board. He was completely misled when he appeared there by persons who identified themselves as members and supporters of Solidarity. I want to discuss some of these things briefly.</p>

<p>His chief adviser was a person who lives in Benoni but expressed grief on behalf of the people of Palm Ridge, and said that his heart lay with the people of Palm Ridge. He is the very person who wrote to the then hon Minister Dookie suggesting that he would offer to serve on <span class="col_577-578" refersTo="page_0311"/>a committee if the hon Mr Dookie would appoint such a committee to be served on by him and the chairman of the management committee of Germiston, because he felt that Palm Ridge was a big enough area to house the entire overflow of Benoni, and there was no need for another area. This is the same person who was the chief adviser of the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition at the hearing.</p>

<p>His lieutenant on that day of the hearing was a doctor who identified himself as being from Solidarity, whereas he himself is a total radical. He is opposed to the whole system and situation of the present Government. In addition, he looks for opportunities. I want to tell you about the last time he made an appearance in Benoni, or on behalf of the people of Actonville. It was some years ago, when he succeeded in acquiring a piece of land from the management committee of Benoni, which was of his preference. He acquired it at a price much less than the upset price, and on conditions which were very abnormal, in that they were contrary to correct procedure and very much in his favour. This was done on the pretext that he was going to put up a clinic for the people of Actonville. He has now erected a shopping centre on that site. That is the doctor that was his lieutenant.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTER&#x2019;S COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>May I ask the hon member whether that property was purchased from any State department?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#lambat">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A E LAMBAT</person>:</from>

<p>Yes, Mr Chairman.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTER&#x2019;S COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>I have a second question. Is the hon member not amazed that that is not listed in any allegations?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#lambat">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A E LAMBAT</person>:</from>

<p>No, Mr Chairman. It is very surprising, and that is why I want to know why nobody mentions it.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I crave the hon member&#x2019;s indulgence; would he take a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#lambat">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A E LAMBAT</person>:</from>

<p>Will the hon member give me some of his time?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>Yes. The hon member said that some doctor &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#lambat">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A E LAMBAT</person>:</from>

<p>The hon member must not waste my time. I have plenty to say.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>He did agree to take the question. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#lambat">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A E LAMBAT</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I am trying to tell hon members that I will take no further questions.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon member is not prepared to take a question.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>He is making an accusation that he cannot defend.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#lambat">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A E LAMBAT</person>:</from>

<p>These are not accusations, Mr Chairman. Anyone can come up with the facts, and these are things that can be seen and heard. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>The indication was that Windmill Park be developed for the well-to-do and the rich people. My colleague the hon member Mr Abram read out the application. And that a small piece of land belonging to the HNP be developed for the poor.</p>

<p>This, then, is the type of apartheid which this lieutenant of the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition wants to create in Benoni. We cannot stand for that.</p>

<p>I in particular wrote a letter to the Deputy Minister. Not only did I write a letter, I had an interview with him. I told him the following:</p>

<block name="quote">In view of the fact that you are contemplating advertising the Windmill Park and Apex areas, may I also suggest, earnestly and humbly request, and plead with you, to simultaneously advertise the Leechville and Larrendale areas? They are the most suitable areas for immediate annexation to Actonville, and this will afford great relief to the Actonville community in view of all the prevailing circumstances.</block>

<p>These areas, Leechville and Larrendale, are the closest adjoining areas to Actonville.</p>

<p>Before coming to the rejected land which is surrounded by slime dumps, do you know what the Minister replied?</p>

<p>The hon the Minister writes:</p>

<block name="quote">As far as Leechville and Larrandale are concerned I have decided that the areas will be utilised for the White population group. I therefore request that no further representation be furnished to me in this regard, as the decision is final.</block>

<p><span class="col_579-580" refersTo="page_0312"/>This is what the hon the Minister tells me and the irony of it is that this prime land must be left to the Whites because the Minister is not prepared to liaise with us about it, but that little piece of rejected land&#x2014;surrounded by slum area and which belongs to an HNP member who could not dispose of it to the Whites&#x2014;that very area is now being dumped onto the Indian community. The hon the Leader of the Official Opposition now asks why we do not wish to accept that land. That HNP member stands up and says: &#x201C;I respect that the Indian community want to live in its own area. They want to be away from the White community&#x201D;. He was actually applauded by the Solidarity members and one of them even wanted to offer him a chair. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Will hon members please give the hon member a chance to deliver his speech.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#lambat">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A E LAMBAT</person>:</from>

<p>The hon the Leader of the Official Opposition attended an <i>ad hoc</i> committee meeting in Boksburg, convened by a Boksburg councillor who was subject to an inquiry concerning bribery and corruption connected with the Windmill Park area. [Interjections.] The hon the Leader of the Official Opposition knew very well on whose behalf he was attending that meeting&#x2014;his general, a man who purports to be a member of Solidarity whenever Solidarity comes to Benoni, but who also purports to be an NPP member whenever they come to Benoni. Yet when the elections come, he is suddenly a radical, a member of the TIC. [Interjections.] A document went to the Attorney-General in connection with&#x2026; [Interjections.] If the hon the State President can take a tomato, then a shoe is an honour for the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition. [Interjections.] This general of the Solidarity party goes and takes options in Windmill Park and prices rise from R20 per square metre&#x2014;as it would have been sold to Whites&#x2014;to R30 per square metre for Indians. Houses valued between R40 000 and R60 000 increased to between R70 000 and R170 000.</p>

<p>This is the situation for the Indian community. Why would the White community not want that piece of land? It is all very well to say that it is a very nice piece of land, but what has happened there? A big prison has been erected there and that is why the Whites do not want it. A hostel has been planned there for the near future, which will house at least 20 000 single Blacks. However, the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition comes and tells us that it is free enterprise. I call it captive enterprise, where the Indians have no choice but to pay the Indian price and not the White price. Is this what the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition advocates for our community? [Interjections.] He tells us to accept any land, but why should we live in the bundu while there is prime land which we want but which our community cannot get? We are not given the land we want, but we are told to take any land we can get.</p>

<p>Is this the kind of land the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition wants us to have? [Interjections.] I want to say that the Actonville housing committee, to which the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition referred, held a public meeting which not even 200 members attended whereas we held two public meetings in the Benoni town hall which more than 1 500 people attended. It is all on video tape, Mr Chairman, and we have a written mandate from over 2 000 people. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

</debateSection>

<debateSection name="#coup_d&#x2019;etat_in_bophuthatswana">

<heading>COUP D&#x2019;ETAT IN BOPHUTHATSWANA</heading>

<summary>(Statement)</summary>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, may I have your permission to issue a statement to this House on behalf of the hon the State President?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon the Chairman of the Minister&#x2019;s Council may proceed.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>I am making this statement, Mr Chairman, on behalf of the hon the State President. It reads as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">As stated this morning by the Minister of Foreign Affairs, the South African Government views the events at Mmabatho in a serious light, particularly because President Mangope and his Government were recently reelected constitutionally as the legal Government of Bophuthatswana. The Minister of Foreign Affairs of Bophuthatswana, Mr S L L Rathebe, together with several other Ministers of the Bophuthatswana Government and their families, as well as officials of the Bophuthatswana Government, took refuge in the South African Embassy this morning. The Bophuthatswana Minister of Foreign Affairs telephoned <span class="col_581-582" refersTo="page_0313"/>the South African Minister of Foreign Affairs from the embassy at 09h00 this morning and said that he and several of his colleagues had decided unanimously to make an appeal to the South African Government for assistance. His message read as follows:</block>

<block name="quote">We resolve in the name of peace, justice, stability and the protection of the lives of people and property, and in the general public interest that assistance be sought from people of goodwill and understanding who would also be supportive of the same principles and therefore call on the South African Government for assistance.</block>

<p>Further information from the South African Embassy is that Mr Malebana-Metsing came to the Chief Justice of Bophuthatswana with armed personnel and insisted on being sworn in as President. The reaction of the Chief Justice was that he could not do so in terms of the constitution. Thereupon Mr Malebana-Metsing declared himself to be President and the Chief Justice merely certified his signature. After this had been done Mr Malebana-Metsing arrived at the gate of the property of the South African Embassy and handed over a message to the South African Government in which he informed the South African Government that a new government had assumed power in Bophuthatswana. In his message Mr Malebana-Metsing says that he will co-operate fully with the South African Government and maintain good relations with the Government of South Africa. Mr Malebana-Metsing also says that South African interests and citizens will enjoy protection.</p>

<p>This morning the SSC met under my chairmanship to go into all the facets of this matter. It was clear to the Government that Mr Malebana-Metsing had taken power in an irregular and violent manner. The South African Government is opposed in principle to the obtaining of political power by violence.</p>

<p>The South African Government recognises President Mangope&#x2019;s government as the government of Bophuthatswana and decided consequently to heed the appeal by that country&#x2019;s Minister of Foreign Affairs and his colleagues to come to the assistance of that government. Moreover, an agreement between the two governments which provides for the one government asking the other for assistance in cases of this nature, as been in existence between the two governments for several years already. It should be mentioned as well that President Mangope, according to the available information, is in detention together with certain seconded South African officials.</p>

<p>Consequently, the South African security forces have been instructed to accede to the request of the Government of Bophuthatswana and ordered specifically to give the highest priority to the security and protection of the South African Embassy and all the staff and their families as well as to President Mangope, his Cabinet and their families and government officials and their families. The security forces are engaged in carrying out their instructions at present.</p>

<p>It should also be pointed out that this case cannot be compared in any way with the recent change of government in the Transkei. In that case no violence was used; neither the Prime Minister nor the Minister of Foreign Affairs of the Transkei appealed to the South African Government for assistance; the constitutionally-elected head of state was not ousted from office and he has, in fact, been recognised by the new government and requested to remain in office as part of the governmental authority; there was no competing entity vying for the exercise of governmental authority; it was clear soon after the announcement by General Holomisa that the new government was in effective control of the country and the national administration; and the South African Government deliberately waited for some time before granting the new government recognition and it was granted only after the Head of State of that Government visited me personally and requested that such recognition be granted.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, the message from the hon the State President is a matter of gravity and I think it is right that this House should associate itself with the action which has been taken by the hon the State President, which is the only proper course a State President of this country could have followed in the circumstances.</p>

</speech>

</debateSection>

<debateSection name="#debate_on_motion_of_no_confidence_in_the_ministers_council">

<heading>DEBATE ON MOTION OF NO CONFIDENCE IN THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</heading>

<summary>(Resumed)</summary>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, hon members will <span class="col_583-584" refersTo="page_0314"/>forgive me if I do not comment on the speech made by the hon member for Actonville. The fact of the matter is that not only was I unable to follow his speech on account of his very fast delivery, but also I could not understand the thrust of the arguments used therein. However, I would like to correct two bits of information on which that hon member seems to be misinformed.</p>

<p>The first one relates to the founding of Solidarity. I would like to assure him that Solidarity was formed in my office, by the hon the Minister of the Budget, my benchmate, the hon member for Reservoir Hills, the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition, myself and some other hon members. Most certainly the hon member for Actonville and the hon nominated member Mr Abram had absolutely nothing to do with the founding of Solidarity.</p>

<p>The other point that I would like to clarify for the benefit of the hon member for Actonville is that when this House was first constituted, Solidarity had two representatives from the Transvaal. One was the hon member for Central Rand and the other was the former hon member for Eastern Transvaal, Mr Arbee.</p>

<p>Having said that, I would just like, with respect, to correct the impression which has been created by the hon member for Southern Natal. He is partly correct, but I would like to give another interpretation to the argument advanced by him. I think he said that if this House were to agree by a unanimous substantive motion that there should be a commission of inquiry into all the irregularities that we have been hearing about, then the hon the State President is honour-bound to appoint such a commission of inquiry. That is only half-true, because as all hon members will recall, this House has, in fact, taken several such unanimous decisions, without avail. Looking at the hon the Minister of Health Services and Welfare, I am reminded of the fact that this House unanimously decided that something needed to be done about the foreign-trained doctors. Of course we know that nothing happened in that regard.</p>

<p>If one looks at the Commissions Act, No 8 of 1947, one realises that in terms of that Act, certainly in terms of section 1 of that Act, the criterion by which the hon the State President ought to and must appoint a commission of inquiry is the criterion of public concern.</p>

<p>Can anyone in this House deny that there is public concern regarding the various allegations that have been made in this House? Can anybody deny that we have had many, many editorials which have been written on this matter? We even had the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council reiterate that he is not opposed in principle to the appointment of such a commission of inquiry.</p>

<p>Because it is the hon the State President&#x2019;s right to appoint a judicial commission of inquiry, all hon members in this House will remember that I first raised the issue when the hon the State President came to this House in support of his Budget Vote last year. For the benefit of some hon members who were not with us at that time I would like to read out what I said on that occasion. I quote from Hansard: Delegates, 20 August 1987, col 2352 as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">As far as housing and land allocation are concerned, the hon the State President is aware that serious allegations have been made against his Cabinet colleague, as well as against a former member appointed by him to the Ministers&#x2019; Council of this House. That former hon member has himself called for such an inquiry. All of these accusations and counter accusations serve only to tarnish the image of this House and the image of the hon the State President&#x2019;s administration.</block>

<p>I went on and said the following:</p>

<block name="quote">When it comes to the Indian community, when matters of grave public concern affecting the entire community give rise to calls for the matter to be impartially investigated under the full glare of public scrutiny, I believe it falls on deaf ears, and I believe that the hon the State President owes it to the Indian community to appoint such a commission of inquiry.</block>

<p>I make bold to say this afternoon that I believe that the hon the State President is shirking his responsibility towards this House, towards the Indian community and in particular towards his hon Cabinet colleague.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#naranjee">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M NARANJEE</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, may I ask the hon member a question? I would like him also to read the hon the State President&#x2019;s reply. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>I will inform that hon member as to what the hon the State President said, seeing that his memory is not too good. The hon the <span class="col_585-586" refersTo="page_0315"/>State President did not react to my call for a commission of inquiry into the allegations relating to land deals. All he did was to react to my call for a commission of inquiry into educational matters.</p>

<p>I believe the hon the State President is shirking his responsibility to this House, and the question that arises is: Why is this the case? Is it because one of the men involved is his Cabinet colleague and political ally? I believe the hon the State President needs to explain the situation.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>You are in the dock in the eyes of God!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>To react to the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council. I am always in the dock when it comes to the question of being judged by the Almighty, and so is the hon the Minister and so are all of us. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>Mr Chairman, you will recall that you spoke about the dignity of this House and about the dignity of office in this House the other day. Hon members will recall that all of this derives from the traditions that have come down to us from the mother of all Parliaments, namely Westminster. These constitutional and parliamentary traditions and conventions are especially enshrined in our new Constitution by section 88. I think all hon members should be aware that it is a parliamentary tradition in all respectable systems that if there is even a mere whiff of corruption or maladministration and an official of the administration is involved or is alleged to be involved, that official or occupier of high office, if he has any self-respect, first resigns.</p>

<p>He either resigns until the matter is cleared up, or he immediately asks for the appointment of a commission of inquiry so that his name can be cleared. In this country we have such a tradition. A precedent was set when Mr Fanie Botha resigned because there was a whiff of a scandal.</p>

<p>In the light of what I have just said, I in all respect and humility call upon the hon Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council either to petition the hon State President immediately to appoint a commission of inquiry, or failing this, I call upon him to resign immediately out of respect for this House and especially for his fellow colleagues and out of selfrespect to himself.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Is that for the Press?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>No, that is for you.</p>

<p>The motion before this House is a motion of no confidence in the Ministers&#x2019; Council. How can any member sitting on this side of the House have any confidence in the Ministers&#x2019; Council when the Chairman himself has no confidence in or respect for this particular council. He has no respect for or confidence in his colleagues.</p>

<p>We have not forgotten that it was none other than that hon gentleman, who is the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council, who passed a vote of no-confidence in his colleagues last year when he called them simpletons. As far as I can remember, he has neither retracted nor apologised. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>I ask the hon member to quote from that Hansard.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>I shall quote the particular Hansard at the appropriate time by writing to the hon the Minister tomorrow.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Quote the page numbers.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>I shall quote you, and I shall also refresh your memory by telling you that it was your hon Chief Whip who drew your attention to it.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#baig">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M Y BAIG</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, may I put a question to the hon member?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I shall always take a question from that hon gentleman.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#baig">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M Y BAIG</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, will the hon member for Springfield agree that after the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council had used the word simpleton, I reminded him that it was most probably a slip of the tongue. It was not said deliberately and he said so and this is accordingly recorded in Hansard.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I am indebted to the hon Chief Whip for confirming that that is what his hon leader said in this House. [Interjections.] Please do not waste my time.</p>

<p>I would again like to remind the hon Chief Whip what a simpleton means. According to the Oxford <span class="col_587-588" refersTo="page_0316"/>Dictionary a simpleton is a halfwit. It is a person who is easily deceived. It is a fool. In view of this how can I, or anybody in this House, have any confidence in the members of the Ministers&#x2019; Council?</p>

<p>Were I to be in a court of law I would rest my case, but this is not a court of law, it is a House of Parliament and we are debating a vote of no confidence in the members of the Ministers&#x2019; Council. It is a motion that reflects, and is based, on the council&#x2019;s inability, its integrity and its incompetency to deal resolutely and purposefully with the various crises that face not only the Indian community but all of South Africa.</p>

<p>I want to remind this House once again that it was none other than the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council who said, on behalf of his party and most certainly I believe on behalf on himself and his Ministers&#x2019; Council, on the first day we took office here (House of Delegates, Vol 2, 1985, pp 18-19):</p>

<block name="quote">Our dedicated task is not to tighten the bolts and nuts of apartheid but to use every opportunity in this House and every opportunity in other structures of this newly created Parliament to loosen the nuts and bolts of apartheid.</block>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>[Inaudible.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>In reaction to my learned friend the hon member Mr Nowbath, all I find is that he has obviously loosened his nuts.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>You are plain nuts.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>I want to remind this House that it was the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council who proposed to effect this great achievement according to the principles enunciated by Mahatma Ghandi. The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council cannot deny that. They are noble sentiments. I joined him in support of that.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>He will never recognise our progress.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>Let us examine whether these glibly mouthed sentiments have been translated into practice. Let us examine for instance the integrity and the performance of the Ministers&#x2019; Council in this regard. We all know that apartheid is structured.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>We are not flying in the sky.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council says that he is not flying in the sky; he is flying in the clouds.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>We are not flying in the clouds.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>He is flying in the clouds and it is about time the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council had his two feet planted firmly on the ground.</p>

<p>Let us measure the integrity and performance of the Ministers&#x2019; Council in this regard.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>Do you have anything new to say?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>Apartheid is structured on the principle of separation. I say this because I would like the hon member Mr Nowbath to know this.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>What is this House? Where are you sitting?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>That principle manifests itself in three important pieces of nasty legislation. The first is the Group Areas Act, the second is the Reservation of Separate Amenities Act, and the third is the Population Registration Act.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Are you not in an apartheid chamber?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>Let us examine the record of the Ministers&#x2019; Council with regard to these statutes. We had a notice of motion only this afternoon from, I think, the hon member Mr Abram.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Are you prepared to rid our society of the Mafia?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council has asked me whether I am prepared to rid the Ministers&#x2019; Council of the Mafia.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>I did not say from the Ministers&#x2019; Council. That is unparliamentary. I said from society.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>If the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council gives me the assurance that he <span class="col_589-590" refersTo="page_0317"/>will get rid of the Mafia relating to this Ministers&#x2019; Council, I will assist him in ridding society of the Mafia.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>That is unparliamentary.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>I have serious business to discuss and the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council is only trying to divert my attention, to no avail.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">
