<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Which building contractors go bankrupt regularly?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>Not only has the NPP not taken any stand on these basic acts&#x2014;the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council knows this&#x2014;it has made no representation in the Cabinet in which the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council is a member without portfolio.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Tell us which contractors went bankrupt and who masterminded them.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Is the hon member for Springfield prepared to take a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>He is wasting my time.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon member is not prepared to take a question.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Which supplier committed suicide?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>I say I have no confidence in the hon the Chairman of the Minister&#x2019;s Council or in any of his Ministers because they have generally acquiesced in this policy of the NP Government.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Who is Tokkie Saayman?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>Ask the hon the Minister of the Budget and he will tell you who Mr Tokkie Saayman is?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister_of_the_budget">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER OF THE BUDGET</person>:</from>

<p>He will blow the whistle.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>People keep asking me what the difference is between the policies of the NP and the NPP.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>The quality of the Chairman!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>And I keep telling them: &#x201C;Nothing&#x201D;.</p>

<p>I said to members of the NPP that &#x201C;NPP&#x201D; stands for &#x201C;National Party&#x2019;s People&#x201D;. That is what they are.</p>

<p>It is in this regard that I believe the Ministers&#x2019; Council has failed. It has failed the community.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>We should move a motion of no confidence in you.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>They have failed because they have hitched their wagons onto the caravan on the NP policies &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>That&#x2019;s for the Press; those are the usual cliches.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>&#x2026; which are leading this country to the cliffs of disaster. By doing that, the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council is taking a lot of the Indian community towards that cliff of disaster together with the NP. [Interjections.] I should like to make another submission this afternoon. It is simply this: From the track record of the Ministers&#x2019; Council it is evident that &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Your track record is dismal.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>&#x2026; this Council has absolutely no vision, and any council without vision is doomed to disaster. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>Regrettably the Indian community will pay a price for the actions of the NPP. It is in fact paying that price in the fields of education and other related fields. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Will hon members please give the hon member a chance to continue.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>How can we have confidence in people whose political conscience closely resembles well-tanned rhinoceros hide? How can we support a Cabinet that has failed to give community leadership at the national and regional levels? The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council is a signatory to the Kwa-Natal Indaba. The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council was a signatory to the Buthelezi Commission.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from><span class="col_591-592" refersTo="page_0318"/>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Yes.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>However, what did you do after signing those reports? Precisely nothing!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>I will tell you. I do not sleep in air-conditioned offices.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>Why is that? It is simply because his political star is hitched to the wagon of the National Party.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>[Inaudible.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>We all know where that is leading us.</p>

<p>What did the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council say when the media of this country were gagged? Absolutely nothing!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>What did you do with the <i>Graphic</i>?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>The silence from the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council was deafening.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>[Inaudible.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>More to the point, Mr Chairman: What did that hon Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council do when the Cabinet decided to drastically cut the subsidies paid to universities? Absolutely nothing.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>No, no. Your facts are wrong.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>Well, then, tell us the facts.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>You will get the facts in the education debate when the hon the Minister of National Education is here. Why do you not tell them what you told the Ministers over lunch tables? [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>Before I resume my seat, I wish to say that I have been given to understand that the NPP, and more particularly the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council, do not have a motto. I, in all humility, would like to suggest a motto to them. That motto should be <i>sumus semper in exertum sed acta variet.</i></p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">F M KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, it would have given me great pleasure if the hon member who just sat down had repeated what he said in English. I am sure that many of the hon members, like myself, do not understand Latin. We came into Parliament with great expectations.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>Charles Dickens!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">F M KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>However, what do we have today?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>Small expectations.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">F M KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>That is right. The hon member can keep it that way. A lot of accusations have been thrown across the floor. I am sure hon members will agree with me that once a person accuses another of misconduct or asks for a commission of inquiry, that person himself had better also be cleared of any past wrongdoings. However, there must be some valid reason for asking for such a commission of inquiry. The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council has challenged certain hon members of this House to repeat such requests openly outside, so that we may continue with the actual commission. At least then we will have some means to ask for it.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>That is no condition.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">F M KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Sure, it is just that those who ask for such a commission should have the courage of their conviction.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>They are hiding in a post box.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">F M KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>On Tuesday 29 January 1985 I asked the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition in this House the following question (Hansard: Delegates, col 137):</p>

<block name="quote">I want to ask one question, Mr Chairman. How did the hon the Leader of the Opposition acquire those two Houses when there were people who were in desperate need for it? How did he acquire it for himself and his workers in Lenasia? The proof for this accusation appeared in the <i>Sunday Times.</i> I was personally involved in the matter, Mr Chairman. The Minister at the time, Minister Pen Kotze, informed us and we have proof of that, that he did not allocate the Houses. The Regional Director of the Department of Communication and Development at that time told us that <span class="col_593-594" refersTo="page_0319"/>it was allocated to the hon the Leader of the Opposition, Mr Chairman.</block>

<p>I have brought this proof today and I would like to hand it to the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, this matter was raised and I think the answers were given in 1985 &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>That is not a question.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>But this is the reason for raising the &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">F M KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>No, ask me a question. Please do not give me a story.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I would like to ask the hon member if the Minister concerned did not somewhere in the newspaper admit that the application was made, considered and granted.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">F M KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>No.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, the hon member should get all the facts.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">F M KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>I am giving the facts.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I suggest that you submit that to an inquiry &#x2026; [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">F M KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>It is funny, Mr Chairman. Suddenly everybody wants to shout me down because the shoe is now on the wrong foot. If they had been so innocent at the time one would never have found a heading of that size in the <i>Sunday Tribune.</i> I would like to give this evidence to the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition, so that he can have a look at it&#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>I do not want to look at it. I am absolutely satisfied that it was a <i>bona fide</i> application which was granted.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">F M KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>This was not a <i>bona fide</i> application because I can quote the chairman of the management committee concerned from this very newspaper article. [Interjections.] One gathers here that the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition approached this man for &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Assistance.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">F M KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>No! To side with him and get houses where there were only 2 000 houses for people who needed those houses. That chairman then took away two houses which were needed for those poor people. Here it is, I have it here.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>You are talking nonsense.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">F M KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>It reads: &#x201C;Jumping the housing queue. Tycoons may get homes in spite of waiting lists&#x201D;. [Interjections.] Where is the sense, Mr Chairman, in his doing a thing like this and then coming here and asking for a commission of enquiry for someone else?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>I want a commission of enquiry into that, too.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">F M KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>The hon member should ask for one for himself. [Interjections.] In 1985 I asked the Chairman that question and he replied: &#x201C;I will answer you&#x201D;. It is now 1988 and I still have not been given an answer. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Well, ask the Chairman; do not ask me.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">F M KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Why should I ask for a commission of enquiry when he is sitting here and he could have given me that answer at any time since 1985? Now all of a sudden he is talking about a commission of enquiry.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, on a point of order: The hon member should go to the right authorities and get his facts straight. Then, when he has the facts straight, let him come and make the accusation. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">F M KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>I will ask the hon member a question.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, may I ask the hon member what he would say if undue favouritism were to be granted to that application?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>A crying shame!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">F M KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>That is a shame because that is exactly what was done. When we were fighting and shouting against the Group Areas Act here, we said how bad the White man was and complained about what the White man was doing to us. However, we forget what we do to our people when we are in power. Why do we oppress our <span class="col_595-596" refersTo="page_0320"/>people? Is that what he is trying to tell me, Mr Chairman?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, is the hon member aware that plots were granted to that same firm in Richards Bay? The reason advanced was that they wanted housing for their employees when at the time the plots were allocated, against the wishes of the community who were waiting for plots, that firm had not yet opened in Richards Bay.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, may I ask the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">F M KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>I have not answered the question, Mr Chairman. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>I have a clean sheet, Mr Chairman. I will stand the test of any enquiry. I have an absolutely clean sheet. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">F M KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>We came here to work for the community as a whole. Since the very first time we asked for a commission of enquiry here I have stated that I would be the first to stand up and say: &#x201C;Put a commission of enquiry onto me&#x201D;. From the days when I was in local government, and even before then when I used to help people, to this very day I have never even approached any official for a home for my own children. That is what one calls honesty; not dealing under the table and obtaining houses for other people and then naming them, like the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council says.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Who dealt under the table?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">F M KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Open yourselves to it, then.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>You are making serious allegations.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">F M KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>I have been asking questions since 1985, and I have been unable to obtain a single answer to them. [Interjections.] That is one of the reasons why we have this motion here today.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Make your allegations outside! [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">F M KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>What is this? [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>I want to sue him for defamation. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Would hon members please restrain themselves. The hon member may continue.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">F M KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>It is all very well for the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition to ask me to go outside and face a law suit. All he will get is my shirt. That is all I have left. [Interjections.] That is all I have left because I am an honest man who did not work to line his own pockets. I did not approach Ministers behind anyone&#x2019;s back for licences. I have worked sincerely for my people and hon members can go anywhere today and ask anyone to point a finger at me.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>What have you done for your people? What is your record?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">F M KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Go and find out. What is the hon member&#x2019;s record? Taking houses under the table? Does he call that a record? [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! I think hon members will agree with me that the dignity of this House is now being lowered. I shall now take firm action against hon members who continue in this manner.</p>

<p>Hon members are allowed to contribute, but not in the manner in which they have now been doing. I therefore appeal to both sides kindly to allow the speaker to continue, but I also appeal to the speaker kindly to address the House in a non-emotional manner.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">F M KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, when a person or persons, regardless of who they are, ask for a commission of enquiry, there must be some substance. Here today I was asked to repeat what I said outside so that people can have a commission of enquiry or sue me. Yet, the irony is that for years people have been asking others to repeat outside what they say inside, so that we can have commissions of enquiry, but unfortunately, nothing is being done about this.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>They are not man enough.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">F M KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>That&#x2019;s it! The hon member takes the words out of my mouth. As he said, they are not man enough to stand up and say that they will do it.</p>

<p><span class="col_597-598" refersTo="page_0321"/>&#x2020;We must be man enough, go outside and repeat what we say inside, so that we can get that commission of enquiry. It is as easy as A, B, C. Do hon members want a commission of enquiry? The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council has openly stated here, that if hon members will repeat their allegations outside, they will have a commission of enquiry. I therefore think it is high time that, because we are protected by Parliament here, we must stop making accusations which we cannot substantiate and cannot repeat outside.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>That applies to yourself.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">F M KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>It does not apply to me; it applies to the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition, because today, after all these years, he could not answer a simple question. Because he is confronted with a piece of paper which says that he jumped the queue, he all of a sudden gets hot under the collar, in such a nice cool building. I cannot understand this thinking. Why ask for something if one knows one is not going to get it, because one&#x2014;I do not want to use unparliamentary language&#x2014;has not got the courage to go and say it outside? Have a commission of enquiry by all means. Let us get it down. Go outside and accuse people of what they have done, and we will have a commission of enquiry. I think it is as simple as that. There is no sense in making a big noise and kicking up a row here, where nothing can be done, and it is only recorded by the press and Hansard.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister_of_health_services_and_welfare">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER OF HEALTH SERVICES AND WELFARE</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, in support of the amendment moved by my colleague, the hon member Mr Abram, I wish to state emphatically that I fully support the leader of my party, the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council, and my colleagues in the Ministers&#x2019; Council. It is not my intention to repeat what was already discussed yesterday and the day before, but I wish to deal with some positive developments in my ministry.</p>

<p>However, before doing so I just wish to respond to the statement made by the hon member for Reservoir Hills. Unfortunately he is not present here. This is an issue which he is very concerned about, and so are we. The statement was made in regard to the Phoenix Hospital. I want to repeat what I said during the 1987 debate on my Budget Vote, namely I would like to give the House an undertaking that this Ministers&#x2019; Council will guarantee that the construction of the Phoenix Hospital will commence in 1989. I would like to inform the House that the hospital will comprise of 750 beds, with building costs to the value of R72 million and equipment to the value of R22 million.</p>

<p>Over and above that, I wish to announce with effect as from 1 April 1988 social relief for indigent Indians will be increased from the present maximum of R12,70 per week per adult to R14,00 per week per adult, and the present R9,00 per week per child under 10 years of age to R9,90 per week per child under 10 years of age. This will now in fact be on par with the White community in South Africa.</p>

<p>I want to prove to hon members the positive steps that have been taken by the Ministers&#x2019; Council. In 1985 the amount was R10,40 per week per adult and this has now been increased to R14,00 which means an increase of 32%. In 1985 the amount per child was R7,30 per week and in April 1988 it will become R9,90 per child per week which is an increase of 36%. This is a positive step and I am sure that the hon members who criticised the Ministers&#x2019; Council will appreciate the positive role that it plays.</p>

<p>During the year under review, it can be reported with confidence on the progress and relevance of the health and welfare divisions in their endeavours to meet the health and welfare needs of our community. Significant developments, policy and aspirations will be referred to briefly. However, I first want to touch briefly on an issue which was not raised earlier.</p>

<p>Hon members are aware of the fact that two days ago we had floods that were similar to what we had in September of last year. I want to inform the House that we have made all possible enquiries and I am pleased to be able to report that no Indian family was really affected. Unfortunately 17 Black families were affected. With the exception of two Indian families in Westcliff, they have been rehoused.</p>

<p>With regard to the Natal flood disaster last year, officials of my department responded promptly and effectively in rendering services and meeting the urgent needs for food, clothing and shelter for the many victims of the Natal flood disaster during September and October 1987 at a cost of some R250 000. The generosity and ready assistance of community leaders and members contributed <span class="col_599-600" refersTo="page_0322"/>immeasurably in alleviating the plight of those affected. It is gratifying to know that the department was able to mobilise resources to combat an emergency situation of this magnitude.</p>

<p>As far as the service areas are concerned, social welfare covers the total spectrum of the wellbeing of the individual, the family and the community. Service areas and functions include: The protection and promotion of stable family life and the child; the welfare and care of the aged and physically impaired; the prevention of alcoholism and drug abuse and the treatment of those affected, etc. The list goes on but one of the important factors which will be of great concern to all hon members and to the Ministers&#x2019; Council concerns social pensions and social relief.</p>

<p>The allocation and administration of social pensions and relief to the disadvantaged sector of our community is undertaken responsibly and objectively with the application of established criteria. Every effort is made to deal with applications courteously and promptly with due respect to the dignity and selfworth of the individual. Basic needs of recipients are of primary concern, and equally important is our responsibility in promoting selfreliance and discouraging dependency.</p>

<p>Despite the foregoing, there is full recognition of the need for a full-scale investigation of the present social pension and relief system. It is outmoded and fails to meet the prevailing problems of economic stress and unemployment. Greater flexibility is required, if provisions are to be relevant, in addressing the situation effectively. Despite severe financial restrictions, it is encouraging to note that recent pension increases are narrowing the gap for all those groups and this department will endeavour to obtain funds with the objective of reaching parity in the next three years.</p>

<p>As far as decentralisation is concerned, I want to touch on the question of the Department of Social Welfare and Pensions. We receive cases every day and I am sure hon members on all sides of the House appreciate that every effort is made to give personal attention to them. Members must appreciate that we cannot give disability grants willy-nilly to every application. Some suffer from coughs or colds or arthritis for that matter, and every effort is being made to ensure that each and every case is treated very compassionately.</p>

<p>It is the policy of the department to decentralise and make its health and welfare services more readily accessible to members of the public. In this regard, offices have been opened in Ladysmith, Stanger and East London. Offices are also being considered for Umzinto&#x2014;as far as Umzinto is concerned things have already got off the ground and the contractors are on site&#x2014;Rylands, Cravenby, Tongaat and Palmridge. The offices in Lenasia are now operating on a full-time basis as opposed to part time previously.</p>

<p>The principle of partnership with the private sector is the basis of the welfare policy. This is one of the ways whereby services can be made more effective in response to needs. Officials of the welfare directorate have enjoyed a close working relationship with the National Councils and many private welfare organisations on all levels.</p>

<p>This is in keeping with the concept of privatisation whereby certain services and functions are undertaken by such organisations. It must be stressed, however, that in areas where the private sector is unable to provide the necessary welfare services, these are undertaken by departmental staff.</p>

<p>A new development in the financing of welfare services is the welfare programme/project formulation. This focuses primarily on the evaluation of community needs, and the programmes to combat them and not simply on the maintenance of existing services and objectives. This system is applicable to the private sector but it is obvious that the department must follow the same principles of programme formulation by objective.</p>

<p>This division undertakes preventative and statutory functions in respect of various Acts, including the Child Care Act, the Criminal Procedures Act, and the Abuse of Dependence-producing Substances and Rehabilitation Centres Act. This work is concerned with the distressing problems of child abuse and neglect, crime, and drug and alcohol abuse. Services include institutional care, statutory supervision, reconstruction services and rehabilitation and aftercare.</p>

<p>As far as the integrated approach is concerned it is most significant to note that there has been a shift in emphasis from an almost exclusively case work concern to a broader integrated approach, which incorporates close up research, groupwork and community development. This involves the perception and determination of need by the <span class="col_601-602" refersTo="page_0323"/>community and the involvement and participation of its members &#x201C;doing with rather than for&#x201D; them. New challenges for effective social development are opened in this way.</p>

<p>I just want to inform hon members about the progress when I spoke in terms of positive steps taken by this Ministers&#x2019; Council. As far as school social work is concerned, where the department has rendered health services to schools for many years, the department is establishing a division of social work in schools as a priority, starting off with four social workers at Phoenix. Here the accent will be on the prevention of and early intervention in social problems affecting both the education of the child as well as being a valuable contact point in identifying family and community problems, for example, growing problems of alcohol and drug abuse of the schoolgoing child, as well as antisocial gang activities.</p>

<p>Hon members will recall that on 23 January 1988 the Green Fields Place of Safety was officially opened. It is a facility in which 78 children could be accommodated. [Interjections.] It is attractively designed and situated in a garden and rural setting and is a facility in which we can take great pride. What is even more encouraging is the provision of the multi-professional team of a social worker, clinical psychologist and occupational therapist, which will be able to assess and treat the children effectively.</p>

<p>As far as the Valley View Place of Safety is concerned the erection of a new place of safety on the existing Valley View site is being proceeded with. It is intended to provide all the necessary facilities and professional services so that children in need of care and management can be catered for on as homely a basis as possible. It is the intention of the department to preserve the original architecture, since the possibility exists that it will be declared a national monument. This will also enhance the image of the Administration. As an interim measure, essential renovations to the existing building are in the process of being undertaken.</p>

<p>I now come to the rehabilitation centre at Newlands West. Under this Ministers&#x2019; Council for the first time the Administration will be able to provide this long-needed facility for the treatment of drug abuse and alcoholism. It is expected that the centre will open in June 1988 and be fully operative by August of this year. It will be staffed by highly skilled professionals of all the disciplines concerned and will contribute immeasurably in addressing these problems.</p>

<p>Members will also recall the taking over of the Phoenix Community Health Centre. The centre is programmed to serve the needs of the local community by providing a nurse-orientated primary health care clinic service five days a week with a 24-hour maternity service component as well as an emergency service. It caters for approximately 600 patients a day.</p>

<p>As far as Actonville is concerned I am pleased to announce that the new multipurpose community health centre is at present being planned alongside the administration block.</p>

<p>In Stonebridge, Phoenix, the Efficiency Services Subdirectorate has been urgently requested to investigate the infrastructure for a multipurpose centre. The building is expected to be ready for occupation during April 1988.</p>

<p>Hazelmere House at Canelands has recently been acquired. Facilities will be provided for psychiatric in-patients and out-patients and will include facilities for vocational rehabilitation. Hostel facilities for 40 patients will also be provided. This facility will enable patients to adapt in the transition phase from an institutional environment such as Fort Napier Hospital to the community.</p>

<p>The relapse rate of patients discharged from Fort Napier Hospital is in the vicinity of 50% and it is considered that this facility will reduce this high relapse rate. A clinic facility for people in the Verulam area will also be provided.</p>

<p>The Phoenix Assessment Centre should come into operation during May 1988. The administration has taken the initiative in establishing this much needed facility. The purpose of this centre is to assess disabled persons in the community as regards their residual functional ability. With the assistance of supplementary health services professionals&#x2014;that is, paramedics&#x2014;nurses and social workers, the disabled will be guided as to their best vocational ability. In this way it is foreseen that the disabled will become productive and independent members of the community.</p>

<p>A suitable site has been identified for a community health centre in Crossmoor, Chatsworth, and negotiations are to take place between the departments of Health Services and Welfare and <span class="col_603-604" refersTo="page_0324"/>Education and Culture with regard to the use of this site for health services.</p>

<p>I have outlined briefly some of the positive steps. In conclusion, the Ministers&#x2019; Council has nothing to be ashamed of. The recent by-election clearly signals the support of the people of Tongaat and the Eastern Transvaal in acknowledgment of the progress in the field of housing, education, and health and welfare services, and a positive approach towards the building of Indian agriculture. This has taken place more under this Ministers&#x2019; Council and the leadership of the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council.</p>

<p>The <i>Daily News</i> editorial of 30 November 1987 confirmed the momentum gained by this House.</p>

<p>Finally, it is now our fourth year in this Parliament and the deliberations in this House ought to be not only meaningful and constructive but also to maintain the dignity and decorum of this House.</p>

<p>One has only to read through Hansard to glean how much time, effort and utterance is expended in unnecessary, trivial and petty matters. I should like to appeal to the hon members of this House to confine their deliberations to matters of a substantive nature that merit the attention of this House.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#pillay">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A K PILLAY</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, we have listened to the speech by the hon the Minister of Health and Welfare. He made a lot of statements, perhaps by way of defence, in the course of his speech.</p>

<p>Mr Chairman, last night I had a call from my constituency. I was asked how the debate was proceeding in the House of Delegates. This took me aback. I told the caller that there were accusations and counteraccusations that dominated most of the time in the House, and that the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council put up a tremendous show. The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council yesterday made the claim, among many others, that he was a very good referee. Undoubtedly he is a very good referee.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Not is, was.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#pillay">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A K PILLAY</person>:</from>

<p>Well, was and is. I have no problem with his refereeing, but when the referee takes over the playing and starts shooting the goals, then there is a problem. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>You want results, and when you get results, it is a problem!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#pillay">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A K PILLAY</person>:</from>

<p>I made an observation this afternoon. When the hon member for Springfield was speaking, the only person that was predominantly reacting from the other side was the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>I asked him: Who is Tokkie Saayman.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#pillay">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A K PILLAY</person>:</from>

<p>Then I looked across and in a sweeping glance I took in all the other hon members, who were either listening attentively and enjoying it, wondering, or completely divorced from the attack.</p>

<p>Now, this is something to reflect on. I pity the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council. He is a one-man show here, and he is putting up a one-man battle.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>That is why you came to this House&#x2014;as that one man.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>[Inaudible.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#pillay">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A K PILLAY</person>:</from>

<p>However, when the referee blows the whistle, he makes a very good job of it. The Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council has mastered this technique to such a degree that he has even a will to get the cockroaches and grasshoppers into his own enclosure. I must admire him for that.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>I beg to differ; somebody yesterday had a better result.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#pillay">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A K PILLAY</person>:</from>

<p>My time is very short. I know these tactics of attacking by the hon the Minister is a defence mechanism and distracts people from speaking so that they will not make their point.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Are you not my ex-guru?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#pillay">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A K PILLAY</person>:</from>

<p>You see, that is another tactic.</p>

<p>The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council boasts of his ability. Yesterday he said that he was in my constituency at the time of the flood &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Yes, twice.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#pillay">

<from><span class="col_605-606" refersTo="page_0325"/>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A K PILLAY</person>:</from>

<p>&#x2026; and that he attended to the people, while the others were not there. I should like to remind the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council that I have no helicopters and aeroplanes at my disposal.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>But you have air tickets at your disposal.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#pillay">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A K PILLAY</person>:</from>

<p>I do not have the advantage or the power to arrange for even a donkey to carry me across. As far as I am concerned that flood disaster was a very serious matter. It was a tragic affair. If any man goes down there to boost his ego and his image and that of his party, then his sincerity is questioned seriously.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>[Inaudible.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#pillay">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A K PILLAY</person>:</from>

<p>One does not talk about others not having been there because one was there oneself. [Interjections.] In any case, if the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council or anyone else was there who could have avoided the misery caused by the disaster, the important thing was to remedy that situation and assist those people, not to indulge in party politics and party propaganda in that particular situation.</p>

<p>Let me tell you that the political intervention developed into a crisis. Let me tell hon members that a crisis between the Crisis Committee and the NPP was averted by the presence of myself and the hon member for Stanger. [Interjections.] It is sad. People have lost a lifetime&#x2019;s belongings in that flood and does anyone think that taking them a cup of coffee and a blanket will solve the problem? I told them to clean up their houses and we would process their claims, but that it would be a long-term solution. I warmed the hearts of those people and they believed in me and trusted me. I did not talk to them about Solidarity. I only told them that as a member of Parliament I was there to help them. It was a catastrophe in the province, so much so that the hon the State President declared it a national disaster and in so doing was able to help.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Who was a Rip van Winkle?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#pillay">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A K PILLAY</person>:</from>

<p>My time is limited, but I can point out a lot of weaknesses in this no-confidence debate. I find the Ministers&#x2019; Council and especially its hon Chairman guilty of a lot of things.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>What happened when you came to my house? Speak the truth.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#pillay">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A K PILLAY</person>:</from>

<p>The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council has this constant habit of inferring that I have a skeleton in my cupboard. Why did I go to his house? I never went to his office or anywhere. He misconstrues things and casts aspersions &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Speak the truth, speak the truth!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#pillay">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A K PILLAY</person>:</from>

<p>It is a shameful thing that in this ruling party its hon Ministers place the party before the community.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>I have a witness. I have a witness.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#pillay">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A K PILLAY</person>:</from>

<p>The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council is like a parrot. That is about all he knows.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I would like to hear from the hon member if he agrees that only an opportunist would try to capitalise on a national disaster.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>They go in late.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#pillay">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A K PILLAY</person>:</from>

<p>Certainly. That is what the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council has done and that is what he is saying now. On Monday, when the new hon member for the Eastern Transvaal was being sworn in, the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council said: &#x201C;One more to come&#x201D;. The tone and the manner of that expression indicated vengeance. That tone reflects his determination to hold power and gloat in his glory. When he achieves success, to me it is a hollow victory. Concerning the Tongaat election campaign, I have teacher friends whose names I do not wish to divulge&#x2014;although I could supply a list as an affidavit&#x2014;and they confided in me about the NPP&#x2019;s electoral operation in Tongaat. I can take a reasonable, sensible defeat any time &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Enjoy your last few months in this House!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#pillay">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A K PILLAY</person>:</from>

<p>However, I will never enjoy a <span class="col_607-608" refersTo="page_0326"/>shallow, hollow victory. Subsequently certain teachers were promoted and I was not surprised to see their names, although I was shocked.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>If you were not surprised, why were you shocked? [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#pillay">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A K PILLAY</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, that was because it confirmed what I had been told. I have no confidence in the Ministers&#x2019; Council and its chairman.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTER&#x2019;S COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>You are my ex-teacher. You are moving a motion of no confidence in yourself. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#pillay">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A K PILLAY</person>:</from>

<p>Yes.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTER&#x2019;S COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>You moulded me, and you have no confidence in me. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#pillay">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A K PILLAY</person>:</from>

<p>I moulded you, but a father can be disappointed in his son, too, on account of what he does. [Interjections.] They are so totally preoccupied with entrenching the concept of own affairs that they have either forgotten to make an input into the reform process in this country or they are turning a blind eye to the views of concerned people in shaping the destiny of the broad mass of people in this country. I say this because I feel sure that everyone in this House, as well as the public at large, is analysing the situation and the contribution we are making to this system. And what do we have to report back to them on? Arguments, accusations and counteraccusations, as well as calls for a commission of enquiry on the one hand and calls against a commission of enquiry on the other. Let us prove our worth. Let us bring about the reforms in this country that will solve the problems of the generations to come. Let us have a sound administration. Let us not proclaim in the newspapers that we have a clean administration, when it is not clean.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister_of_education_and_culture">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER OF EDUCATION AND CULTURE</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, in support of the amendment I want to say that we have had some very good debate here this afternoon&#x2014;some of it comical, some otherwise. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>I want firstly to read something from a study I made in education which is very relevant to the criticisms that are being levelled here this afternoon. Herbert Spencer said: &#x201C;The great aim of education is not knowledge, but action.&#x201D;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#palan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">T PALAN</person>:</from>

<p>Like the strip-tease! [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>I want to tell hon members of the Opposition that the NPP has demonstrated its action in various fields, as mentioned this afternoon and yesterday by my colleagues, the hon the Minister of Local Government and Agriculture, the hon the Minister of Health Services and Welfare and the hon the Minister of Housing. Hon members have heard how much development and action took place during the year. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>Increased facilities were established in all these fields. I do not want to give hon members too many details about them. Those details will be provided in the various Budget speeches.</p>

<p>Another learned gentleman by the name of John Wannamaker said the following:</p>

<block name="quote">Criticism is futile because it puts <i>a</i> man on the defensive and usually makes him strive to justify himself. Criticism is dangerous because it wounds a man&#x2019;s precious pride, hurts his sense of importance and rouses his resentment.</block>

<p>This is exactly what those hon members have been doing. This afternoon we saw the resentment of the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition. When facts are brought before the House, it hurts. It hurts very much indeed. [Interjections.] I should like the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition to indicate in his reply how he was able to acquire plots of land in Richards Bay on the assumption that his establishment, Sea Land Air, would be established there. Sea Land Air was not established. These plots were acquired, although they may not have been acquired during the term of office of my colleague, the hon the Minister of Housing. Whenever it was, it is a fact.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, will the hon the Minister take a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>I am not taking any questions, Mr Chairman. We all have limited time.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>Then he must not give distorted facts.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>&#x201C;Please be short&#x201D;, I have been told. [Interjections.] I am illustrating what John Wannamaker says, and not what John Iyman says. [Interjections.] Mr Chairman, if I may have another say in this context: Truth, like oil, must <span class="col_609-610" refersTo="page_0327"/>flow to the surface. One cannot hide truth. Our leader has made it amply clear that if hon members repeat these things outside this House, they will have their commission of enquiry.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, will the hon the Minister take a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>No, Mr Chairman, but I will say this: I am very disappointed with the hon leader of the PRP, as well as the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition. I am disappointed in that both these hon members missed a very good opportunity to rise to the occasion and pay tribute to the late Mr Munsook who was an hon member of this House. The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council, the leader of the National Peoples Party, did pay this tribute. It is a pity that hon members of the opposition parties did not rise to such an occasion or even aligned themselves with the tribute paid by the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council. It will go down in the history of this House. Our culture demands that we pay tribute to a man who has served in this House. [Interjections.] It is too late.</p>

<p>We on this side of the House appreciate that convention demands that this debate takes place and that the opposition parties put forward all the criticism they want to level against the ruling party, albeit we have not made bones about the fact that there is constant repetition of matters that were dealt with last year, the year before and the year before that.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Is the hon the Minister prepared to take a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>All right, I will.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, would the hon the Minister indicate whether he takes a light view or a serious view of it if a senior official of the department of which he is the head were to encourage people to commit fraud, perjury and falsity?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, it is not relevant to the discussion. [Interjections.] I would take it very seriously. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>Mr Chairman, having just returned from various elections where we swept the board, I know that the feeling of sourness is still there with the members of the Opposition. Hon members from the Opposition have been gloating over their success yesterday, but one swallow does not make a summer. There is a special kind of swallow, a swift. If the hon member makes a swift crossing of the floor, he will no longer be on the other side.</p>

<p>I want to go on to some of the matters mentioned by my hon colleagues on the other side. The hon member for Cavendish referred to promotions, appointments and transfers of educators in my department.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>They were autocratic appointments!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>No, I deny that categorically.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>Have a commission of enquiry!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>The hon member can have that, but I deny that allegation categorically. I want the hon member for Cavendish especially to know that he as an educator realises very well that we have a committee of professional people to do this.</p>

<p>He also made mention of examinations. Much has of course been said about this subject. Our results have been very much better than before.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, may I ask the hon the Minister a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>No, Mr Chairman.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>Is the hon the Minister afraid of something? [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>No, I am not afraid of anything. I will answer the hon member fully when my budget vote is discussed. We will have plenty of time then. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>There has been great satisfaction as far as the matric examination is concerned. [Interjections.] Will the hon member for Stanger please listen to what I have to say?</p>

<p>Mention was also made of the appointment of teachers. I want to say to the hon members here that we discussed it fully last year. Hon members must appreciate that we had a problem. I thought the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition would have made a list of the progress that we made as well as the matters that require attention. It should be the job of the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition to list a number of things that are found wanting. He should assist the ruling party in the House in these matters, and he should also give credit where credit is due.</p>

<p>I will not be able to answer all questions, because <span class="col_611-612" refersTo="page_0328"/>of time limitation, but I want to mention that the hon member for Southern Natal drew our attention to a certain acting principal. I want to tell him that the matter is receiving the attention of the hon the Minister as well as the attention of the Director-General.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodliar">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">C N MOODLIAR</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, it is interesting to note that the hon the Chairman of the Minister&#x2019;s Council is a reputed referee. Being a referee, one needs a great deal of judgement, control, and above all, one must be an impartial official in every football match. I also want to remind him that in the world-famous stadiums there are protection zones for referees who misjudge matches.</p>

<p>I have a respect for the office of the Chairman of the Minister&#x2019;s Council but when the hardy annual of bribery and corruption creeps in, I wonder what is really happening. I am not accusing him in any way because the question of bribery and corruption is a hardy annual in this House. I think we are tired of listening to it and I demand of the hon the Minister to put an end to it. Let us bury it in 1988, either by a commission of enquiry or by any parliamentary machinery that is at our disposal.</p>

<p>I make a very earnest appeal&#x2014;as not only the name of the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council is tainted, but all hon members of this House are surrounded by suspicions&#x2014;that until and unless we bring an end to these allegations of bribery and corruption, all hon members of this House stand accused of being dishonest. It is my demand therefore, that this matter will not be debated in this hon House.</p>

<p>I hope that the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council takes whatever steps he can that could lead to a commission of enquiry. I am ignorant as far as these steps are concerned, but I often hear repeated statements that the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council requires those who are accusing him, to make their statements outside the House. However, I think adequate publicity has been given to this matter in the Press and outside.</p>

<p>I now want to dwell on a very controversial topic, and that is the M L Sultan Technical College.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#thaver">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M THAVER</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, is the hon member prepared to take a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodliar">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">C N MOODLIAR</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, may I inform you that I am not taking any questions and that that learned &#x201C;attorney&#x201D; can sit down.</p>

<p>I now come to the very controversial topic of the M L Sultan Technical College. The hon Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council knows that any official in charge of an institution which is run on State funds is subject to certain regulations, particularly with regard to stock. When I was a headmaster we took stock on 31 March every year, and in that stock report all deficiencies, all shortages and other discrepancies were brought to light. The men who were on the stock inspection committee certified that stock record. I am surprised, however, that this very august institution had a deficit of stock running into hundreds and thousands of rands without any control, without any check or without those people who are responsible, being brought to book, or some departmental justice being meted out to those people who are responsible for such anomalies. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>I am sorry, I am not prepared to take any questions. I will give hon members the opportunity when I have a few minutes.</p>

<p>This is a very serious anomaly on the part of the authorities who are running the situation. M L Sultan Technical College is a very august institution in the Indian community, not only in Natal and in South Africa.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Blame the chairman!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodliar">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">C N MOODLIAR</person>:</from>

<p>I am not blaming the Chairman, but I demand of the hon the Minister to put an end to this controversy that is going on at the M L Sultan Technical College. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>I now want to come to another very important point. On Tuesday the hon member for Cavendish quoted the following, amongst other things in his speech, and I want to quote him verbatim:</p>

<block name="quote">There is no need for undue delay in the appointment of the Chief Executive Director. This is not in the interests of Indian education nor the community at large.</block>

<p>That this post has been vacant for a considerable time is common knowledge. The nominated member&#x2014;I have not got his <i>curriculum vitae</i> at my disposal&#x2014;is, I think, a non-practising &#x201C;attorney&#x201D; or he may be a legal assistant, I cannot say.</p>

<p><span class="col_613-614" refersTo="page_0329"/>The hon Mr M Thaver gave the quoted speech a contorted or misconstrued malicious twist by suggesting indirectly that the hon incumbent to the post of executive director sought the opposition&#x2019;s support to expedite his appointment. The hon Mr M Thaver should know that the acting executive director of education could not be present in this august Chamber to defend that allegation.</p>

<p>I leave that to his conscience.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>If he has one.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Will the hon member for Stanger withdraw that statement.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>I withdraw it.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodliar">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">C N MOODLIAR</person>:</from>

<p>One would have expected the hon member Mr Thaver to have made a worthy contribution by reason of the fact that he holds a very elevated position. His position in the House is that of a deputy chairman of committees. I wonder whether he was appointed on merit. I am surprised that this emanates from a man holding the position of deputy chairman. He has made insinuations about the Director of Education who is respected by both Houses. We are responsible and this is the respect we accord a man holding a very important position. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! I should like to draw the attention of the hon member for Phoenix to the fact that the hon the Deputy Chairman of Committees was elected by a resolution of the House.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>But he was nominated.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodliar">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">C N MOODLIAR</person>:</from>

<p>He was nominated, not by my party, and I accept your explanation.</p>

<p>To sum up my whole argument, I have nothing against any member in this House.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#thaver">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M THAVER</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I want you to check in Hansard the address by the hon member for Phoenix where the hon member casts a reflection on the position I hold. I would like you to give me a ruling on it tomorrow.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>I object to the hon member Mr Thaver&#x2019;s rising on a point of order and making a speech. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#naranjee">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M NARANJEE</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I would also like to come to your assistance on this matter to see whether the hon member has reflected on the hon the Deputy Chairman of Committees because we hold the Chair in this House in very high esteem. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! For the benefit of hon members I cleared that immediately with the hon member for Phoenix. He said he wondered whether the hon the Deputy Chairman of Committees had been elected on merit. I pointed out to him that the hon the Deputy Chairman had been elected by the House.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#naranjee">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M NARANJEE</person>:</from>

<p>Later he also went on to ask whether he was worthy &#x2026; [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, is the hon member Mr Thaver permitted to say &#x201C;stupid&#x201D; from the other side?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Did the hon member Mr Thaver use the word &#x201C;stupid&#x201D;?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#thaver">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M THAVER</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I said it was a stupid suggestion. I did not refer to anybody. I admit I said it was a stupid suggestion but I did not refer to any particular person.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodliar">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">C N MOODLIAR</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, may I ask, with due respect to you, whether these people are enjoying a special privilege? I told hon members clearly that I was not taking any further questions.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! What is the hon member for Phoenix suggesting?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodliar">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">C N MOODLIAR</person>:</from>

<p>I think hon members are taking advantage of the Chair. [Interjections.] This is my very humble submission.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! What is the hon member actually suggesting?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodliar">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">C N MOODLIAR</person>:</from>

<p>Hon members are disturbing the Chair and they are disturbing me while I am trying to continue with my speech. Despite the fact that you have implored them to stop, they continue. In my opinion they are taking advantage of the Chair. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon member may continue.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodliar">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">C N MOODLIAR</person>:</from>

<p>Thank you very much.</p>

<p>In conclusion there are three aspects that have <span class="col_615-616" refersTo="page_0330"/>been raised. To sum up they are the corruption inquiry, the question of M L Sultan, and finally to clear the good name of the incumbent holding the position of acting executive director. His name has been cleared and he has in no way sought the assistance of the Opposition in highlighting or expediting his appointment.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I crave the indulgence of the Chair and the members present to give me a fair hearing. Most serious allegations have been made here and I want to state my case clearly so that it may be understood.</p>

<p>When I opened my address in moving the motion of no-confidence in the Ministers&#x2019; Council and the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council, I said that I am always prepared and will be ever prepared to submit myself to any enquiry where there are doubts about my <i>bona fides.</i></p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_members">

<from><person refersTo="hansard_za">Hon MEMBERS</person>:</from>

<p>Hear, hear!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Despite that, the hon member for Lenasia East has run away from here. He made allegations on matters going back to 1981. I just wish to state for the purposes of the record what an official of the Department of Community Development said, namely that Mr Pen Kotz&#x00E9; had instructed him to make the allocation. The Minister approved of it. Mr Kotz&#x00E9; said in his reply that he had not influenced the allocations and that the allocation had been made on merit. There is the answer to his query, Mr Chairman. Unfortunately, that gentleman chose to ignore those important factors contained in the press report and made an issue just to embarass me.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>He knows no better.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>He knows no better, and I am certain that he was encouraged to do these things by somebody in order to try and make capital, as usual. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>The answer is here.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>The answer is here.</p>

<p>In my absence reference was made to my company buying industrial land in Richards Bay. We did not buy it with a grant from anybody&#x2019;s grandfather.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_members">

<from><person refersTo="hansard_za">Hon MEMBERS</person>:</from>

<p>Hear, hear!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>I made the purchase because the land was offered for sale&#x2014;anybody could have bought it! It was industrial land. [Interjections.] I am not taking any questions.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>What about me? I bought land in the same way! [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Secondly, the authorities advertised residential plots which employers could purchase for their employees. I am in the shipping business, and in anticipation of us moving some day to Richards Bay, I bought those plots and we have just taken transfer of them now.</p>

<p>As far as Cato Manor is concerned, when I made the case for Cato Manor we were asking for Cato Manor from Bellair Road near the river right to Clair Estate. There were objections from Whites, who asked what kind of homes Indians would build in this area. All I said to them was that the homes that would be built, would harmonise with the environment. [Interjections.] I want the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council to listen: &#x201C;Harmonise with the environment.&#x201D; Part of that environment is subeconomic homes zoned for Black people.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_members">

<from><person refersTo="hansard_za">Hon MEMBERS</person>:</from>

<p>Hear, hear!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>You are answering Whites.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>You are basking in reflected glory.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>In so far as the reference to Mr Kotz&#x00E9; is concerned, he was not a Minister when I was chairman of the executive committee. My Ministers were Minister Steyn, Minister of Community Development; Minister Heunis, Minister of Planning; and in addition, Minister De Klerk dealt with me. I do not know anything about Mr Kotz&#x00E9;. I wrote to Mr Kotz&#x00E9; and pointed this out to him, and the gentleman accepted that. Therefore hearsay evidence is being used here to castigate people &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>It is not hearsay evidence.</p>

<p>[Interjections.]</p>

</speech>
